# QA Test Report

[A1] Git hygiene - PASSED
What I ran: Read `AGENTS.md` and `SECURITY.md`; inspected Git-tracked paths and `.env` history; scanned tracked content and Git history for common credential/token patterns while suppressing matching contents and excluding `.env` files.
Result: `.env` is not tracked, and Git history contains no `.env` path. No tracked cookie jars, test/browser artifacts, or build outputs were found. High-confidence token/key signatures and long credential-assignment checks found no matches; broad heuristic matches were limited to candidate paths and were not treated as confirmed secrets. No `.env` contents were opened.

[A2] Backend verification - PASSED
What I ran: `./mvnw clean verify` from `backend/springreactdemo/`; the first sandbox attempt could not download the Maven wrapper, so I reran the approved command with network access.
Result: Maven reported `BUILD SUCCESS`; 26 tests ran with 0 failures, 0 errors, and 0 skipped. No JaCoCo report or coverage goal appeared in the build output. Test startup connected to the existing MongoDB at localhost:27017; no database writes were part of this verification run.

[A3] Frontend static checks - FAILED
What I ran: `npm ci`, `npm run`, `npm run lint`, and `npm run build` in `frontend/`; inspected installed top-level ESLint/TypeScript packages and existing `dist/` file names/sizes.
Result: `npm ci` completed (265 packages added; npm reported 9 moderate advisories). Lint failed because ESLint 6.4.0 could not find a configuration; build/typecheck stopped because `tsc` was not found. No unit-test script is configured. Existing `dist/` contains a 1,004,499-byte JS bundle and no `.map` files, but the failed build means these pre-existing artifacts do not verify the current source build or source-map emission.
Likely cause / proposed fix: The frontend scripts reference ESLint and TypeScript tooling that is not available in the installed top-level dependencies, and lint configuration is missing or not being discovered. Reconcile the package manifest/lockfile and lint configuration, then rerun A3; no fix was applied.

[A3] Frontend static checks (rerun) - PASSED
What I ran: Clean-reinstalled the already-declared dependencies; added Vitest as a development dependency, a `test` script, and two API-client unit tests; ran `npm run lint`, `npm run test`, and `npm run build`; checked generated bundle files and the local frontend URL.
Result: ESLint passed; 1 test file and 2 tests passed; TypeScript check and Vite production build passed. JS bundle is 1,004,499 bytes, CSS is 9,660 bytes, and no source maps were emitted. Vite warns that the JS chunk exceeds 500 kB. npm reported 9 moderate advisories during install (not audited here). Only dev tooling/tests, package metadata/lock, and generated `dist/` changed; application runtime source and runtime dependencies were not changed. A localhost:5173 check failed to connect after the reinstall/build; causality is unconfirmed, and the dev server was not restarted pending approval.

[B2] CRUD as ADMIN - FAILED
What I ran: Authenticated as the dev admin; created one `qa-` product, read it, updated its description, deleted it, fetched it again, and checked the product count. The temporary product was removed.
Result: Create/read/update/delete returned 201/200/200/204; the deleted product returned 404 with a sanitized ProblemDetail. Product count returned to 500. The update API did not accept or return a version: `ProductResponse` has no version field and `PUT` performs a read-then-save, so the required version-aware update was not verifiable. No test data remains.
Likely cause / proposed fix: Version is present on the Mongo document but omitted from the API DTO/update contract. Expose a version token and require it on updates, enforce optimistic locking, and add stale-version tests before rerunning B2; no code changes were applied.

[B2] CRUD as ADMIN (rerun) - FAILED
What I ran: Repeated an admin CRUD cycle with a new `qa-` product and supplied `version: 0` on PUT; checked the response shape, post-delete ProblemDetail, and final collection count.
Result: Create/read/update/delete returned 201/200/200/204; create and update responses had no version field. PUT succeeded despite the supplied version, so version concurrency is not enforced through this API contract. Post-delete returned sanitized 404; count returned to 500 and the temporary record was removed.
Likely cause / proposed fix: The request DTO does not declare a version, and the response omits it; the version property in the submitted JSON was not enforced. Add an explicit version-aware update contract and optimistic-locking tests before another B2 run; no code changes were applied.

[B7] Error handling (diagnostic rerun) - BLOCKED

[C6] Read-only investigation of the apparent PUT denial - ROOT CAUSE FOUND
What I ran: Inspected the active filter-chain matchers, CSRF filter, JWT role conversion, controller/service, and existing admin PUT integration tests; did not access `.env` or send runtime requests.
Result: Security configuration allows `PUT /api/v1/products/**` for ADMIN, and the integration test covers successful admin PUT. The prior QA harness invoked `curl --data-binary` without `-X PUT`; curl therefore sent POST to `/api/v1/products/{id}`, which falls through to the default-deny rule and returns 403. This explains the observed status as a harness method error, not an application authorization defect. No application code or product data changed during this read-only investigation.
Proposed fix: Rerun C6 using an explicit `-X PUT` and the version-aware request body, with the same qa-prefix cleanup/count verification. No application code changes are needed.

[C6] Mass assignment (approved contract follow-up) - FAILED (harness assertion; not a confirmed application defect)
What I ran: Started independent unknown-field POST/PUT rejection checks on a new run-owned qa- fixture, with readback checks after each pair. The harness failed before completing C6 and stopped the requested rerun sequence. It emitted only the fixed SAFE_CONTRACT_CHECK_FAILED code, not raw error/request details.
Result: Finally cleanup completed, and an authenticated stable-id traversal verified 500 distinct products, zero qa- names/categories, and no leftover owned data. No 429 occurred. No backend restart, configuration mutation, vulnerability-data download, or service change occurred.
Likely cause / proposed fix: C6 compares a subsequent GET byte-for-byte with the original POST response, including Instant audit timestamps. The mapper returns the in-memory saved entity on POST; Mongo date roundtrip precision and/or object-key ordering can make this comparison fail without a mutation. This explanation is provisional because the safe harness intentionally suppressed raw diagnostics. Proposed correction: capture the initial persisted GET as the immutable baseline, compare subsequent reads structurally including every server-controlled field, and emit only fixed safe failure-stage codes. Also rename the numeric evidence status key to httpStatus so it cannot overwrite PASSED/FAILED. Obtain approval before correcting/rerunning C6, then run E5 and continue the remaining approved steps in order. A2 clean verify and the D2 proposal remain pending; no vulnerability data will be downloaded.

### Proposed D2 commands (NOT EXECUTED)

Installation only, after approval (fresh temporary destination, checksum before execution):

```bash
qa_osv_dir=$(mktemp -d /tmp/spring-react-osv.XXXXXX) &&
qa_osv_release=https://github.com/google/osv-scanner/releases/download/v2.7.0 &&
curl --fail --location "$qa_osv_release/osv-scanner_linux_amd64" --output "$qa_osv_dir/osv-scanner_linux_amd64" &&
curl --fail --location "$qa_osv_release/osv-scanner_SHA256SUMS" --output "$qa_osv_dir/osv-scanner_SHA256SUMS" &&
(cd "$qa_osv_dir" && sha256sum --check --ignore-missing osv-scanner_SHA256SUMS) &&
chmod u+x "$qa_osv_dir/osv-scanner_linux_amd64" &&
"$qa_osv_dir/osv-scanner_linux_amd64" --version
```

SBOM generation only, after separate approval, from the repository root (plugin/dependency metadata downloads possible; no test or app launch):

```bash
./backend/springreactdemo/mvnw -f backend/springreactdemo/pom.xml \
  org.cyclonedx:cyclonedx-maven-plugin:2.9.3:makeAggregateBom \
  -DincludeTestScope=true -DoutputFormat=json -DschemaVersion=1.6 \
  -Dcyclonedx.skipAttach=true
```

Scan only after inspecting the SBOM and obtaining advisory-query approval. Use the verified scanner's actual temporary path, not an unrelated PATH executable. Planned invocation (the installation-stage variable must still refer to that verified directory):

```bash
"$qa_osv_dir/osv-scanner_linux_amd64" scan source --no-resolve \
  --lockfile=backend/springreactdemo/target/bom.json --format=json
```

The scan's stdout can be captured by a credential-safe runner under ignored target/ after approval. No --offline/automatic database-download flags, autofix, or scan-serving HTTP listener will be enabled. The scanner's own help must confirm the pinned binary's flags before any networked scan. Installation approval alone does not authorize the SBOM or scan commands above.

| Assessment | Finding and evidence | Proposed disposition, NOT APPLIED |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Medium design-hardening candidate | 26 EI_EXPOSE_REP/EI_EXPOSE_REP2 reports: Product getters/setters (Product.java:105,109,113,117), DTO collection components (ProductRequest/ProductUpdateRequest/ProductResponse/ProductPageResponse/AuthUserResponse), User roles (User.java:28,44) and injected collaborators (ProductController:42, ProductSeeder:56, ProductService:41, AuthService:36). Review DI warnings separately; do not blindly copy Spring collaborators or make proxy-dependent classes final. |
| Low hardening candidate, exploitability unproven | CRLF_INJECTION_LOGS at api/ApiExceptionHandler.java:101 logs request URI with an exception. Parameterized logging does not itself escape line breaks; parser/container rejection may constrain input. DM_CONVERT_CASE at the same file:108 uses default-locale lowercasing in ProblemDetail type creation. | Consider bounded CR/LF-safe URI logging and Locale.ROOT, with log-injection/locale regression tests. Do not change logging or production behavior without approval. |
| Informational/expected design hotspots | 11 SPRING_ENDPOINT, one SERVLET_PARAMETER, one SERVLET_HEADER and four COOKIE_USAGE reports identify controllers/request inputs/cookie reads. SecurityConfiguration request authorization, typed validation and CSRF checks must be considered; a hotspot is not proof these protections are missing. | Retain the existing security tests and document individual source/dataflow reviews before any narrow suppression. No CSRF/CORS/cookie protection should be removed. |
| Expected dev-only behavior | Two PREDICTABLE_RANDOM reports at ProductSeeder.java:71,72 refer to the explicitly reproducible fixed-seed product generator, not password/token generation. Seeder is dev-profile-only. | Preserve reproducibility; consider only narrow seeder-specific justification after approval, not replacing the required deterministic fixture generator. |
| Low/in-process hardening review | Three CT_CONSTRUCTOR_THROW reports (AuthService.java:32; LoginRateLimiter.java:28,32) and two THROWS_METHOD_THROWS_CLAUSE_BASIC_EXCEPTION reports for SecurityFilterChain factory methods. Constructors intentionally reject invalid configuration; Spring configuration methods legitimately propagate framework exceptions. | Review finalizer/proxy constraints and expected framework signatures individually. Do not remove startup validation or broadly suppress constructor warnings. |
| Likely false positive in application code | security/detect-unsafe-regex at frontend/src/pages/ProductFormPage.tsx:151 flags the anchored price pattern with bounded repetitions (up to 12 whole digits and 2 decimals). No unbounded nested repetition appears in that pattern. | Keep client validation intact; a focused adversarial-input test and narrow documented disposition can support suppression if approved. No source change was applied. |
| QA-only security hotspots needing individual review | Remaining 64 frontend warnings cover response/token-redaction regexes, comparison/canonicalization dynamic keys, dynamic test regular expressions, and fixed repository-resolved log/checkpoint filesystem paths. Examples: concurrency.mjs:29/34, restart-idempotence.mjs:75/85/86, support/mongo-user-metadata.mjs:80, product-form.e2e.ts:16, support/safe-reporter.ts:7. | Review each flagged trust boundary; keep secret redaction and every existing assertion. Any regex/suppression changes require approval and regression tests. These heuristics do not establish a real NoSQL/prototype-pollution/path-traversal exploit. |

### I1 codification priorities — proposals only

1. Preserve existing tests and close high-value backend HTTP gaps in I4: explicit alg=none, real login-to-429, unknown-user/wrong-password response equivalence, operator login bodies/typed queries, and exact-origin/method/header CORS preflights. C1, C2 and strict unknown-field rejection already have committed regressions; strengthen precise omissions rather than duplicate or weaken them. Complete B3/B4/B7/C8/C9 edge/error/leak contracts as separately scoped additions.
2. In I2, first show the Testcontainers dependency/configuration/isolation plan. Current required dedicated test URI is safer than a development fallback but does not meet Docker-only/no-database-environment readiness. Do not assume Docker/image prerequisites or download/start anything under I1 approval.
3. In I5/I6, add the approved component tests/coverage separately from browser coverage, and separate Vite-only HMR checks from Node-only nonce/proxy checks. Agree a safe clean-state/limiter strategy before three consecutive suites; do not silently retry, sleep out windows, weaken the limiter or reset the development database.
4. Commit deterministic wrappers for the six manual-only items: A1 tracked/history hygiene, D1 npm audit, D2 Maven SBOM/OSV/outdated checks, D3 secret scanning, F3 bounded performance measurement, and H3 documentation contracts. A1/D3 can share one reviewed implementation. Networked vulnerability audits should be an explicit opt-in workflow, not an unannounced download during normal offline verification. Real scanner services and acceptance judgments are not replaced by parser unit tests.
5. Also preserve portable scripts currently missing for the partial items: non-disruptive A4 preflight, actual D4 production-bundle scan, G4 before-state capture, C9 scoped log capture/scan and H2 bounded passive assessment with positive controls. Selectively migrate sanitized tooling only; never force-add the ignored QA tree, logs, HAR captures or credential-bearing artifacts.
6. I7 should compose the appropriate deterministic suites with nonzero exits. Keep disruptive G3/G4, load tests and networked audits explicitly gated. I8 should place the durable traceability/results summary and commands in root TESTING.md. Human approvals, exact-snapshot dispositions, architectural recommendations and semantic documentation review remain intentionally manual.

### I4 implementation batches: proposed, not approved or created

**Batch A — JWT/login abuse (recommended first).** New security/AuthAbuseSecurityIntegrationTest.java, with real Boot MVC/security/DAO authentication/JWT/limiter and mocked UserRepository/ProductService boundaries, @ActiveProfiles("test"), explicit owned-container import and disabled request/response printing. No dependency or production changes.

1. unsignedJwtIsRejectedBeforeProductService — runtime-built alg=none cookie returns exact 401 ProblemDetail and never invokes products; valid signed-token positive control.
2. staleSignedCookieAllowsCsrfAndLoginButStillRequiresCsrf — expired and signature-tampered signed variants permit CSRF bootstrap and valid login, while missing/wrong CSRF still yields 403; obtain a usable fresh access cookie without printing it.
3. loginThrottlesTheSixthAttemptForOnePeer — first five wrong attempts are 401, sixth is exact 429 ProblemDetail; parameterize spoofed forwarding-header variation, keep socket peer fixed and prove an independent peer still reaches authentication. No real network, sleeping or shared runtime limiter changes.
4. unknownUserAndWrongPasswordReturnIdenticalProblems — same generic 401 type/title/status/detail/field set, no submitted values or internals; assert securely without echoing sensitive actual values.
5. operatorObjectsAndUnknownFieldsInLoginAreRejectedBeforeAuthentication — object-valued username/password operator payloads and extra roles/id fields return sanitized 400, never 500, mint no access cookie and do not consult the user repository.

**Batch B — CORS/HTTP contracts.** New security/WebSecurityContractIntegrationTest.java for real Boot configuration and filter-chain assertions; new security/AuthCookiePolicyTest.java for dev/non-dev cookie-construction units; extend existing security/SecurityConfigurationTest.java only with configuration guard units. Keep full Boot owned-container isolation; no mocked security bypasses or YAML overrides that would hide configured origin regressions.

1. unsafeEndpointsRequireMatchingCsrfMaterial — real POST/PUT/DELETE and login/logout missing/wrong/duplicate rejection plus matching positive controls; retain existing standalone tests.
2. configuredDevelopmentOriginsHaveExactCredentialedPreflights — localhost:5173 and 127.0.0.1:5173 allowed exactly, no wildcard, configured methods/headers reflected appropriately.
3. disallowedOriginMethodOrHeaderFailsCors — evil.example origin, PATCH method and unapproved header are rejected with 403/no permissive origin headers; preserve current CORS-filter response contract rather than inventing a new one.
4. securityHeadersProtectAuthenticatedAndErrorResponses — nosniff, DENY, authenticated no-store, application-level absent Server/X-Powered-By; HSTS on secure requests, not required on plain local HTTP.
5. healthIsPublicAndShowsOnlyStatus — real Boot health from owned Mongo; response contains only status, not details/components; no production outage operation.
6. managementAndDocsPropertiesRemainRestricted — only health exposure, show-details never and test-profile springdoc disabled; retain existing anonymous/admin access denials.
7. unexpectedServiceFailureHasSanitizedHttpProblem — runtime-generated internal marker is absent from exact 500 ProblemDetail fields/body; no exception names/traces/credentials echoed.
8. issuedCookieLifetimeMatchesJwtAndProfilePolicy — verify actual issued JWT exp-iat matches Max-Age in memory, plus direct dev/non-dev cookie policy (HttpOnly, SameSite, Path, secure outside dev, clearing on logout) using runtime opaque fixtures; no expected Secure flag in dev.
9. corsConfigurationRejectsMixedWildcardsAndMissingNonDevOrigins — add fail-closed configuration cases without broadening allowed origins. Signing-key startup guard and limiter capacity/cleanup branch tests are useful follow-ups, but require their own explicit addition to this list rather than silent scope expansion.
